# MyCity App ERP · Progress

**The single record of what is built.** 105 screens (96 in the staff app, 9 on the public site) plus
the M0 foundation. Every change that moves a screen or a foundation item forward updates this file
in the same commit. An AI assistant does this as part of finishing a task (CLAUDE.md, step 5); a
person does it by hand. Nobody should have to ask "where are we on X".

Screen names and tab slugs are the approved inventory (MILESTONES §18, approved 12 October 2026).
A tab's slug is a URL and does not change when its label does.

---

## How to update this file

1. **Tick the columns** the change completed, on the row of each screen it touched:

   | Column | Ticked when |
   |---|---|
   | API | Every endpoint the screen calls exists with its contract, permission check and tests |
   | UI | The desktop screen is built against the real API (no mock data in the component) |
   | Mobile | The mobile shell is built (gate 11: a native mobile layout, not the desktop one shrunk) |

2. **Set Status** to one of: `Not started` · `In progress` · `Built` (all three ticked) ·
   `On staging` (deployed and demo script walked) · `Accepted` (signed off at the milestone demo).
3. **Set Updated** to the date, day-first: `13/10/2026`.
4. **Leave a note** under the module's **Notes**, newest first, one line per change:

   ```
   - 13/10/2026 · LA · My Attendance: API for the month view and the export; table toggle still to do.
   ```

   Say what was done and what is left. Name a blocker if there is one. Replace `_None yet._` with the
   first note.
5. **Update the summary** counts below when a Status changes.

A row is never deleted. A screen that is dropped by agreement gets Status `Dropped` and a note
saying who agreed and when.

pages. Settings → Notifications was brought forward to M0 by TB on 19/10/2026 and is counted with
M1, like the other M0 screens.

- 08/11/2026 · GE · (Ose) The backend jobs' PDF tests timing out (documents-import, worker: `TimeoutError ... waiting for the WS endpoint`, two runs of 08/11/2026): Chromium's first launch on a fresh runner builds the font cache before it answers, 10.8 s instead of 1.3 s here with 335 fonts and past puppeteer's 30 s on the runner's thousands. Fixed three ways: the backend jobs build the font cache and start Chromium once before jest (ci.yml); `services/pdf/chromium.js` waits 90 s for a launch, tries once more if that runs out, and gives Chromium no D-Bus session to wait on; the worker test allows its child's cold start. `tests/unit/pdf-chromium.test.js`. Servers already build the cache (`ops/provision` `fc-cache -f`). Left: nothing.
- 06/11/2026 · JP · CI in about six minutes instead of 21 (GE's three approvals, and the six-minute aim): the long jobs are split into shards run side by side, each set planned by the scope job (`scripts/check/lib/scope.js` `plan()`). jest runs in six backend jobs, each with its own PostgreSQL and Redis (`MYCITY_JEST_SHARD`, `jest --shard`), its files dealt out by expected cost (`tests/shard-sequencer.js`; the integration files were 937 s on one CPU); the vitest files run in shards per workspace (`MYCITY_FE_TESTS`, `scripts/fe-test.js`: ui 2, app 3, public-app 3 riding with app's), dealt by size (`scripts/lib/deal.js`; vitest's own `--shard` gave one shard app's three slowest files); typecheck, lint, builds and the mock check have their own job (`--except=fe-test`, new in `ci-local.js`), with TypeScript's incremental state cached and dated 1970 on restore, so a stale state is never taken as up to date (`incremental` set in the workspaces' tsconfigs: warm typecheck 6 s against 51 s cold here); the screen gates run per target (`MYCITY_SCREEN_TARGETS`, `MYCITY_A11Y_TARGETS`), dealt over jobs: the gallery's axe pages over four (`MYCITY_A11Y_SHARD`, new in `a11y.js`), the app's screens over four for the crawl and axe alike (`MYCITY_SCREEN_SHARD`, new in `lib/screens.js`). A pull request runs only the sets its files can affect (BACKEND_GUIDE §8.4's table; `app/` alone skips packages/ui's and public-app's tests and the gallery, the backend alone skips every frontend job, packages/, scripts/, root config, the lockfile and .github/ run everything); a skipped job starts no runner and `ci` counts it as passed; a narrowed run leaves no tree, so `main` runs everything after its merge. `npm run ci` on a laptop sets none of the variables and still runs every gate, whole. `tests/unit/ci-scope.test.js` holds the rules and the facts they stand on. Left: GE to review the draft PR; shard counts to tune from a week of job times; the runner-minute cost (more jobs, each its own install) to watch once the repository is private (2-core runners, billed minutes).

- 27/10/2026 · JP · CI wiring, local runs as CI's (JBS Praxis, after the CI history of 24 to 27/10): the backend suite ran out of memory on Node 20 ("JavaScript heap out of memory" at about 2 GB, GitHub run 141), which machines on Node 22's larger default heap never showed; Jest now replaces its worker once it has grown past 1 GB after a file (`workerIdleMemoryLimit`, `jest.config.js`), the files still one at a time. `npm run ci` refuses a Node major other than `.nvmrc`'s, and with `RUN_DB_TESTS=1` says at once when PostgreSQL or Redis is not answering (a stopped database cost a ten-minute run that failed every integration file). A Claude Code cloud session starts as CI (`.claude/hooks/session-start.sh`, SessionStart): Node 20 first on PATH, PostgreSQL 16 and Redis running with the test database, `RUN_DB_TESTS`, `DATABASE_URL` and `REDIS_URL` set (a plain run there skipped the 53 integration files), and the image's Chromium for the screen gates, which the Stop hook also finds; while an `npm run ci` is running (`node_modules/.cache/mycity/ci-local.pid`) the hook leaves the screen gates to it, after a rebuild by the hook took a running crawl's `index.html` away. CI's PostgreSQL logs fatal errors only, so a red backend log ends with its failing test, not thousands of lines from the transaction probe. `dump.rdb` is ignored. From the same history (JBS Praxis's three answers): under the test runner the mock answers every request after the same 90 ms, so answers arrive in the order asked (four frontend failures came from tests reading one answer before another under its random delay, which a browser keeps; FRONTEND_GUIDE §9.5); in `ci.yml` a run that does no work has its own concurrency group and never cancels one that does, a cancelled run reports `ci` cancelled instead of failed (`!cancelled()`), and the screens job's Chromium install tries twice, capped, then fails saying so; a description edit re-runs the document gates alone once a full run on the head passed this tree (`scope.js` `passedRun`, `tests/unit/ci-scope.test.js`). - 20/10/2026 · TB · System Health fault #1 on staging ("Maps are not answering." once on `GET /maps/search`, build `b301312`): Geoapify gave no answer within 4.5 seconds, or the connection dropped; not a defect of the address work, but it alerted as an error and turned the field to its outage state on one blip. Fixed with TB's four answers: the server asks a dropped connection once more at once (never a timeout) and logs its network code; the field asks a failed search or pin once more after 400 ms before it calls search down; the provider not answering is recorded as a `warning` (counted, never alerting), while the `maps` check and Settings · Integrations carry an outage (`docs/guides/addresses.md` §6.5, `system-health.md` §6); and, TB's fifth answer, System Health pushes only `fatal` and `error` faults: a new, returning or spiking `warning` or `notice` (a 422) is counted on Faults and no longer pushed, as §6 already said of notices. Left: a fault's fingerprint holds the release folder, so every deploy files a recurring fault as new and Came Back cannot fire across releases (System Health, proposed separately).

- 21/10/2026 · TB · Direct Messages (#16) and Channels (#15), from TB's phone recording on staging: an open conversation blinked "No messages yet. Say hello." and a photo was refused with "Too many requests". Cause: the conversation answered every receipt with a delivered mark and every mark sent another receipt, so one screen (or two) acknowledged in a loop and spent the person's per-minute request budget. Fixed both ends (`docs/guides/community.md` §3 step 10, §6, §7): the server emits `direct_thread.delivered` and `direct_thread.read` only when a mark covered a message it had not covered before, a delivered receipt goes to the other person only; the app says delivered only for a new message from the other person, the list only when a conversation's newest message changes, and reads a conversation again quietly (nothing blinks, several pushes make one read). A started conversation left empty is in nobody's list and Forward To offers the colleague as a person (§3 step 7, as the guide always said). Uploads count against their own 60 a minute, not the API's, and `UploadField` gains Retry on a failed file. Conversations scroll as on WhatsApp (§3 step 20): 25 at a time as the person scrolls up, keeping their place; the first unread under "N Unread Messages" (up to 4 pages read at once); no jump while reading older messages, with the Newest Messages arrow and its count; the day's label held at the top. Community integration suite 33 of 33 (receipts, empty conversations), unit tests for the two budgets, the thread against a stand-in server and Retry. Left: walk it on a phone on staging with two people.

Forward To searches the channels the person may post in, their conversations and every colleague (their own chat as You), up to 5 at once (`GET /community/forward-targets`, `POST /community/forward`, 20 a minute). **Notes**

- 02/11/2026 · JP · Goals (#29) and Scorecards (#30), target changes tell the people they concern (JBS Praxis, 02/11/2026, from staging: "when the CEO changes a target, no notification is shown about a target change"; `docs/guides/performance.md` §6.7, §7, §14, guide first): the fan-out left out whoever acted, and a change the CEO asks for waits on the CEO alone, so the engine's Approval needed never reached them and the bell held only the 3 day reminder and the overdue notice. Now the engine tells the CEO of a request they opened themself (`include_actor` may be a function; true only for the CEO, the one person `approvers.canAct` lets decide their own); whoever asks is told **Target change sent** (not the CEO, who has the notice above); an approved change tells a line's employee **Your target changed** (`scorecard.line_target_changed` had no notice) and a key result's accountable and co-accountable people **Target change approved**; each names the key result or line, the new target and its month (`changeLabel`, which also reads *October 2026*, not `2026-10`, and 2, not 2.0000). A refusal tells only whoever asked. No migration, no contract change, no screen change. Tests: `workflow.test.js`, `performance-plan.test.js` (3 new, 1 extended; they fail on the old code), `events-workflow.test.js`. The Performance chapter (Change a target, Change a line's target, Decide a plan) and `notifications.md` §6, §11, `foundation.md` §7 follow. Left: every other approval the CEO opens and decides themself now tells them (a plan they send, a policy, their own leave), by decision; the engine's Approval needed for a stage nobody can be told of is GE's (guide §13)

- 28/10/2026 · JP · Goals (#29), KPIs (#31), Analytics (#33) and Home (#1): everyone in General Management, Operations & People and the Implementation Team (JBS Praxis) read the whole plan, and may share and edit it (JBS Praxis, 28/10/2026, P27, amends P25 and P26; `docs/guides/performance.md` §6.25). Answers, asked with `AskUserQuestion`: who is everyone in General Management plus the Operations & People and Implementation roles wherever their department is; what is the whole plan, with the authority to share and edit it. Operations & People are given `goals.view_all`, `goals.company` and `goals.organisation` by role (seed `2542_seed_goal_plan_operations.sql`, editable on Roles & Permissions); General Management is the CEO's department (found from the CEO's employee record, so its name and code do not matter) and its people hold the same three derived in `grants-cache.getCapabilities` (`GENERAL_MANAGEMENT_CAPABILITIES`), so a joiner has them at once and a leaver loses them within the 30-second cache; the session's `capabilities` carry them, so the screens offer New Goal, Share Goal and Share Objective; a function's own goals stay its head's, approvals stay with their approvers, and the share notice is not sent to anyone who already reads the whole plan. The mock mirrors both. Tests: `performance-goal-scope` (Operations & People and a General Management Technical Assistant read, share and edit; moving out of the department takes it back; renaming the department changes nothing), and the role-default lists, Goals, Analytics and Home tests that assumed Operations & People read less. Left: nothing known; the printed training guide `docs/manual/performance-guide/` is not yet updated for 27/10 and 28/10.
- 28/10/2026 · JP · Goals (#29), KPIs (#31), Analytics (#33) and Home (#1): each person reads their own department's goals, OKRs and KPIs, the CEO and Management read all of it, and the CEO and Management can share a goal or a company objective (JBS Praxis, 28/10/2026, P25 and P26; `docs/guides/performance.md` §6.23, §6.24). Until now everyone with Performance read the whole tree. Decided with JBS Praxis: the CEO and Management (the new capability `goals.view_all`, given to Management and the Implementation Team; the CEO holds every one) read the organisational goals and the company's objectives and key results; Operations & People read every function's OKRs and KPIs but the company's only when shared; everyone else reads their own department's (the one on their record, and any they head or act for) and any key result they own or co-own anywhere (shown alone, with no score); a function key result names the company key result it feeds by code and statement and nothing more. **Sharing**: the CEO shares an organisational goal and Management a company objective with its key results, with All Staff or chosen departments, read only (`PUT` and `DELETE /performance/goals/:id/share` and `/objectives/:id/share`, `goal_share` and `goal_share_department`, migration 2540, seed 2541); a share tells the people it newly reaches once (the bell opens it), Stop Sharing tells nobody. One module, `goals.visibility.js`, works out a reader's reach and gives every read the same SQL: Goals, KPIs, Analytics' OKR figures (the company score is the whole plan's alone), the plan, unbind and target change sheets, key results and their trace, ⌘K, the assistant's `goals.okrs`, `goals.trace` and `kpis.list`, Home's Goals This Quarter, Org KPI and Goals On Track; a record outside the reach is 404. On screen: the Function select lists what the reader reads and is left off for one function (its plan is then the one in view), the first figure reads Function Score where Company Score is not theirs, the Organisational Goals card shows only goals there are to read, Share Goal on a goal's sheet and Share Objective on an objective's menu and sheet, a Shared column and pill for the sharer. Tests: `tests/integration/performance-goal-scope.test.js` (10, real migrations; it found a null department read as unknown, not no), the performance integration tests that assumed everyone reads everything now say who reads what, `Goals.test.tsx`, `Analytics.test.tsx`, `Approvals.test.tsx`. The Performance chapter and its pictures, Quick Start's. Left: a company objective is shared quarter by quarter (each quarter's objectives are new records); a share names departments, so a person moved to another department reads what that one is shared with from then on; sharing to named people is not built (decided: All Staff or departments)

- 10/11/2026 · JP · M6 PR 6, AI, search, Home and notices (`docs/guides/payroll.md` Appendix A, `m6/ai-search`): no screen row moves and no status changes, because no screen was built (Appendix A's PR 6 has none; PR 7 is LA's). Praxis AI reads payroll as the screens do: the runs, a month's headcount and (with the salary grant) gross, deductions and net, the payslips and deductions within a Payroll reader's reach (masked as the screen is, Restricted for a role that hides pay), and, for each person, their own payslips and deductions with their amounts (their own pay is never masked from them); it draws six cards a person confirms after Confirm It's You (New Run, Submit for Approval, Add Adjustment, Accept Deduction, Waive Fine, Record Advance), each through the function the screen's button calls, never a person's own fine or advance; the advanced switch is a route to Configurations, not a card. ⌘K finds a payslip (a Payroll reader any, a person only their own, opening My Payslips), a deduction and the New Run action. A submitted run tells the CEO by the bell, by push and by email, each link opening the run; Home's Payroll In Progress card (live since PR 2) still counts a run until it is paid. Left: PR 7 (the screens, which answer `action=new` on Payroll Runs for the New Run action and the route "run payroll"), PR 8 (staging seed, User Guide).
- 10/11/2026 · JP · M6 PR 5, Configurations and the advanced option (`docs/guides/payroll.md` Appendix A, `m6/advanced`): Configurations (#109) has its API, so API is ticked and it is In progress, and its screen, built in this pull request at JBS Praxis's request (as Deductions was in PR 3). `/payroll/settings` reads the setting and changes it from today or later with `payroll.configure` (the pay day, Bank Details Required, Switch On or Off with the three conventions; a second change for the same day joins it); `/statutory` lists the seven items in brief and the rates, and enters a version of an item from a day (PAYE as a whole table of bands); `/salary-structure` sets a grade's band from a day, places a person in a grade (Below Minimum, Within Band, Above Maximum: flagged, never blocked) and sets a person's pay split (adding up to their monthly gross). Statutory is built and shipped off: a run is advanced when the setting in force on its month's first day says so; it then prints the split's lines and computes employee pension, NHF and PAYE as deductions and employer pension, ITF and NSITF as employer charges out of the net, from the rates in force on its last day, with No Rates Set (no PAYE table or a convention unset) blocking Submit and No Pay Split warning. The Statutory Report of an approved advanced run is a restricted PDF per item and per person with the base, the amount and the tax and pension numbers. No rate, band, relief or grade is in any seed: MyCity enters them from confirmed law; the tests carry labelled test rates. The screen (`app/src/features/payroll/Configurations.tsx`, desktop and phone, its mock starting empty as production does) shows Payroll Options, Statutory Items and PAYE Bands always, and Conventions, Bands by Grade, Grades and Pay Components once the option is on or has been. UI and Mobile stay unticked until the screen is checked on the real API on staging (F14 opens it there now). Payroll Runs (#45) has all its API but stays unticked, as before, so its prototype does not open on staging before PR 7. - 09/11/2026 · JP · M6 PR 3, deductions and advances (`docs/guides/payroll.md` Appendix A, `m6/deductions`): Deductions (#48) has its API, so API is ticked, and its screen, built in this pull request at JBS Praxis's request. `/deductions` lists one page of waiting attendance deductions, fines and advances (25 a page, pending first and then the newest; status, source, person, month and search filters; sort by date, person or amount), gives the four figures and the open month, accepts a fine for this month or spreads it over two to twelve (equal shares, the spare kobo to the first month, the start month the open month or later), waives it with a reason, sends an accepted fine back to waiting, records a salary advance and cancels one recorded in error. Never your own; nothing is taken back once a month of it is in a run (`INSTALMENTS_STARTED`); nothing is added to a month whose run is already with the approver. The person is told when a deduction is scheduled or a fine waived, and payroll is told when a sanction is issued with a fine. The screen (`app/src/features/payroll/Deductions.tsx`, desktop and phone, on the contract, its mock in the contract's shapes) has the figures that open their lists, status chips, a source switch, the record beside the list on desktop and in a sheet on a phone, and the forms on the contract's schemas.   something is down to intelligence@jbspraxis.com, copying tomblakeasaah@gmail.com and
  praxisjbs@gmail.com. Fixed: `outbox-dispatch` was never recorded (so it always read as not run),
  a nightly job read as late before its first slot, a run that failed on its first try kept no
  failure time, and Status failed outright while the database was down. Left: install the two

- 20/10/2026 · TB · System Health split into three tabs (#95 Status, #96 Performance, #97 Faults)
  after TB walked the first release on staging, and the engine behind it fixed. **The three
  schedulers were never registered in `src/jobs/registry.js`, so nothing ran for an hour and the
  Performance card read "Nothing Recorded Yet", which looks like good news.** Now: the schedulers
  are registered; every tick is recorded in `schedule_run` (migration 9110); a new `schedulers`
  readiness check reports any job that has not run within three of its own intervals; and
  `scripts/check/schedulers.js` fails the build when a file in `jobs/schedulers/` is not started by
  the worker, which is the gate that would have caught it. Status gains the resources people ask
  for first: disk, memory, processor, and the database's size and connections. Performance now
  shows response time and the share of requests that failed, from `request_stat`, a row a minute
  written by the API (the snapshot runs in the worker and cannot read the API's own metrics), and
  says plainly when nothing is recording instead of showing a neutral empty state. Left: thresholds
  for disk and memory are first guesses and want a week of real numbers.

  writes, with a status the team shares. Ticking faults downloads one Markdown brief that tells an AI
  how this repository works and requires 4 to 10 clarity questions before any fix; the ticked faults
  become Being Handled so nobody fixes one twice. Its own `system_health` permission key, seeded to
  the Implementation Team and the System Administrator only, so HR, Management and the CEO are never
  alerted about a stack trace. Backend: contract, migration 9100 (`error_group`, `error_group_note`,
  `health_snapshot`), seed 9190, eight routes, three schedulers (roll-up and snapshot every 5
  minutes, the 08:00 digest, the nightly purge). Notices sit in a new locked System Health category:
  the bell and push cannot be switched off, email only on the 24 hour escalation, through a new
  per-notice `channels` default on `defineNotification` (`docs/guides/notifications.md`). 30 unit
  tests and 19 integration tests on the real migrations. Left: walk it on a phone once staging has
  it; `ops/monitoring` still alerts by WhatsApp for infrastructure, which is unchanged.
